```
1	# AGENTS.md
2	
3	Guidance for AI coding assistants working in this repo. Read this before
4	editing biological process code, composite wiring, or the type system.
5	Humans should read [CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md) instead.
6	
7	## What this repo is
8	
9	v2ecoli is a whole-cell *E. coli* model. It ports 55 biological processes
10	from [CovertLab/vEcoli](https://github.com/CovertLab/vEcoli) onto
11	[process-bigraph](https://github.com/vivarium-collective/process-bigraph)
12	and [bigraph-schema](https://github.com/vivarium-collective/bigraph-schema).
13	The biology must match upstream unless a PR explicitly justifies a change.
14	
15	## Frameworks you must understand
16	
17	### process-bigraph
18	
19	- A **Composite** = processes + stores (state) + wires (edges from ports to
20	  store paths). Composites are assembled in `v2ecoli/composite*.py` and the
21	  corresponding `generate*.py` files build them for each architecture.
22	- A **Process** declares `inputs` and `outputs` schemas and implements an
23	  `update(state, interval) -> update` method. Updates are applied to the
24	  shared store after each timestep.
25	- A **Step** runs to convergence within a timestep rather than stepping
26	  through time. Departitioned fuses partitioned requester/evolver halves
27	  into Steps.
28	- Don't reinvent composition patterns — copy from an existing process in
29	  `v2ecoli/processes/` or `v2ecoli/steps/` and adapt.
30	
31	### bigraph-schema
32	
33	- Types describe the shape of stores and ports. Project-specific types live
34	  in `v2ecoli/types/`.
35	- Types register serializers via `@_serialize.dispatch`. A save state that
36	  cannot round-trip through the serializer is broken. Test this.
37	- Units are carried as `pint.Quantity` values; schemas enforce dimension.
38	
39	For deeper questions about either framework, invoke the `pbg-expert` skill.
40	
41	## Checks you must apply when adding or editing a process
42	
43	1. **Schema round-trip.** `serialize(state) -> JSON
…[truncated 14519 chars]
```
</details>

## [2026-06-08T15:23:22.772Z] assistant

Before I commit hours of work to this, I need to clarify scope. Here's what I've found:

**Current state**
- `vEcoli_trna/vEcoli` is on `CovertLab/vEcoli@trna_charging_final` (HEAD `330ee3f4`). It's ~20 commits and ~158 files ahead of `origin/master`, with the headline change being a kinetic tRNA charging model (`_trna_charging.pyx`, 638-line Cython), full rewrite of `polypeptide_elongation.py` (3844 vs upstream's smaller version), tRNA-AA integer mapping, ParCa re-optimization, plus validation datasets (Dong 1996, Jakubowski 1984, tRNA synthetase kinetics).
- v2ecoli **already has** a partial port: `processes/polypeptide_elongation.py` (1811 lines), `processes/polypeptide/kinetics.py` (627 lines), `processes/parca/trna_charging.py` (299 lines), plus PRs #110/#117 already split out the polypeptide subpackage. So this isn't a green-field port — it's a refresh/completion.
- Notable missing from v2ecoli: the Cython `_trna_charging.pyx` core, the three validation TSVs, and likely the most recent ParCa re-optimization deltas.

**Why I'm pausing**
A faithful refresh-and-validate is genuinely multi-day work: re-derive the diff on top of v2ecoli's polypeptide/ subpackage refactor, port the Cython kernel (or its NumPy equivalent), regenerate the ParCa cache (hours of compute), and run the behavior + parity + report gates. I'd rather not pick a path without checking the scope with you.

## [2026-06-08T15:35:55.135Z] assistant

Audit complete. Branch created, audit doc written to `workspace/investigations/trna-charging-final/audit.md`. Reporting findings.

## Findings

**The work is bigger than it looked.** v2ecoli's existing port is the steady-state ppGpp/aminoacyl synthetase model. `trna_charging_final` adds a **brand-new `KineticTrnaChargingModel`** (`polypeptide_elongation.py:2198` upstream) on top of that — a per-ribosome codon-tracking kinetic model whose engine is a 638-line Cython kernel (`wholecell/utils/_trna_charging.pyx`). It's an *additional* model, not a replacement.

**What's already in v2ecoli:**
- Steady-state charging + ppGpp regulation (`polypeptide/kinetics.py`)
- The polypeptide subpackage refactor (PRs #110, #117)
- ParCa scaffolding including `trna_charging.py` Step
- Most ParCa `trna_charging_reactions*.tsv` flat files

**What's missing for kinetic charging:**
- The 638-line Cython kernel `_trna_charging.pyx`
- `KineticTrnaChargingModel` class itself
- Brand-new 1276-line `reconstruction/ecoli/dataclasses/relation.py`
- 5 new ParCa flat files (incl. `trna_charging_kinetics_solutions.tsv` with 5688 rows of fitted params)
- Entire `validation/` tree (3 TSVs + `validation_data.py`)
- Library/sim_data/initial_conditions tRNA-kinetic plumbing (~340 lines of deltas)
- Composite wiring + behavior test

**Sized:** 8–12 person-days of careful work; ParCa is hours of additional compute.

## I need three decisions before I keep porting

These are calls I shouldn't make for you because they pin downstream layout and how PRs land:

## [2026-06-08T15:45:12.215Z] assistant

I've made progress on Phase A.1 (data file ports). Stopping here to give you a real status check before continuing — because everything downstream is significantly higher-risk and longer.

## Status

**Done this session (Phase A.1 — verbatim data copies):**
- Branch `trna_charging_final` created locally.
- `workspace/investigations/trna-charging-final/audit.md` — full file-by-file audit (status legend, line counts, destinations, sizing).
- `v2ecoli/validation/ecoli/flat/` directory created with `__init__.py` files; three validation TSVs copied verbatim:
  - `dong1996_table_5.tsv` (2.4 KB)
  - `jakubowski1984_table_3.tsv` (1.1 KB)
  - `trna_synthetase_kinetics.tsv` (10.7 KB)
- `v2ecoli/processes/parca/reconstruction/ecoli/flat/`: copied verbatim:
  - `trnas.tsv` (47 rows — supersedes whatever was there)
  - `trna_charging_kinetics.tsv`, `trna_charging_kinetics_curated.tsv`
- `…/flat/optimization/`: created and copied:
  - `trna_charging_kinetics_constants.tsv` (11.8 KB)
  - `trna_charging_kinetics_solutions.tsv` (5.1 MB — the big ParCa-fit solutions table)
  - `trna_synthetase_dynamic_range.tsv`

**Not yet committed** — per AGENTS.md I don't commit without your explicit ask.

## What the audit revealed that I need to flag

1. **`relation.py` is the next blocker.** v2ecoli has a 145-line `relation.py`; upstream `trna_charging_final` has 1421 lines. The +1276 line delta is the new tRNA↔codon↔synthetase relation logic. It must land before `validation_data.py` or `KineticTrnaChargingModel` can import — a single large careful port (probably 1–2 hours just for this file alone, with import rewrites for `unum → pint` and the v2ecoli-nested ParCa paths).
2. **The Cython kernel is the bottleneck for behavior tests.** Until `_trna_charging.pyx` is translated to NumPy/numba, no behavior test for the kinetic model can pass. That's ~638 lines of low-level per-ribosome loops + numerical solvers — genuinely 1–2 days of careful work.
3. **trna_charging_kinetics_solutions.tsv is the ParCa output, not input.** It's 5.1 MB of fitted parameters that were *produced* by the upstream tRNA-charging optimization run. Whether we trust it as-committed or regenerate from a fresh ParCa run is its own decision.

## My recommendation

A full session-by-session plan looks like this:

| Session | Work | Compute |
|---|---|---|
| **This one** | Phase A.1 data file ports (done) + audit (done) | minutes |
| Session 2 | Phase A.2: port `relation.py`, `validation_data.py`, `validation_data_raw.py` + small ParCa dataclass deltas (transcription, two_component_system, molecule_groups, knowledge_base_raw, simulation_data, getter_functions) | minutes |
| Session 3 | Phase B: port Cython kernel to NumPy/numba + behavior unit-test parity vs upstream golden | hours |
| Session 4 | Phase C: implement `KineticTrnaChargingModel` class + new `kinetic_charging_baseline` architecture + behavior test + composite wiring | hours |
| Session 5 | Phase D: library/sim_data + initial_conditions deltas, smaller process deltas, ParCa pipeline wiring | hours |
| Session 6 | Phase E: run ParCa pipeline (compute) + behavior tests + parity gate + reports | many hours |

32	
33	    def __init__(self):
34	        """Load raw data from TSV files"""
35	        for filename in LIST_OF_DICT_FILENAMES:
36	            self._load_tsv(os.path.join(FLAT_DIR, filename))
37	
38	    def _load_tsv(self, file_name):
39	        attrName = file_name.split(os.path.sep)[-1].split(".")[0]
40	        setattr(self, attrName, [])
41	
42	        rows = read_tsv(file_name)
43	        setattr(self, attrName, rows)

## [2026-06-08T16:03:39.885Z] assistant

Phase A.2 is complete. Stopping here for a real status check before continuing.

## What's done this session

| Phase | Work | Status |
|---|---|:-:|
| A.1 | Verbatim data files: 24 validation TSVs, 6 ParCa TSVs (incl. 5.1 MB optimization solutions), validation dir scaffolding | ✅ |
| A.2 | `relation.py` 145→1421 lines (+`_build_codon_sequences`, `_build_codon_based_translation`, `_build_codon_dependent_trna_charging`, `_build_trna_charging_kinetics`); `validation_data.py` + `validation_data_raw.py` ported with import rewrites; `knowledge_base_raw.py` loader entries added | ✅ |

All 4 modified/new Python files pass `py_compile`. Audit doc at `workspace/investigations/trna-charging-final/audit.md` (211 lines now) has the full status log appended.

## Blocking issue I hit

`v2ecoli/__init__.py` line 8 does `from pbg_superpowers.composite_generator import …`, but `pbg_superpowers` isn't installed in `.venv/` and isn't on disk anywhere under `/Users/arnabmutsuddy/projects/`. `pyproject.toml` declares it as a dep but the venv is missing it. This blocks:
- Runtime smoke-tests of the ports I just did
- ParCa cache rebuild (Task #9)
- Fast tests (Task #10), behavior tests (Task #11)
- Reports (Task #13)

This is independent of the tRNA work — needs `uv sync` or equivalent.

## What's left (sized)

| Task | Effort |
|---|---|
| #2 Cython kernel `_trna_charging.pyx` → NumPy+numba | 1–2 days |
| #3 Refresh `polypeptide_elongation.py` + add `KineticTrnaChargingModel` class | 2–3 days |
| #4 Other process deltas (initiation, transcript_elongation, monomer_counts, etc.) | 1 day |
| #5 Library/sim_data/initial_conditions deltas | 1 day |
| #6 ParCa dataclass small deltas (transcription/two_component_system/molecule_groups/etc.) | 1 day |
| #8 Full ParCa run | hours compute (after venv fixed) |
| #9–13 Cache rebuild + tests + parity gate + reports | half-day each, in order |

## [2026-06-08T19:05:27.837Z] assistant

Good question — and the honest answer is the "1–2 days" was sized as a single-developer continuous push. For session-based work it splits naturally along function boundaries.

## Why I estimated 1–2 days

638 lines of Cython, 580-line companion test, and the kernel uses `libc rand()` for stochasticity — which needs careful rerouting through `numpy.random.RandomState` to keep `seed_rng` semantics deterministic. The three biggest functions are stateful inner loops over per-ribosome codon positions, so they need a careful translation pass plus parity verification before you trust the result.

That sizing was for one person working straight through. It conflated "the work" with "how to package the work".

## Why it absolutely splits

The kernel is 11 mostly-independent functions, not one monolith. Sized:

| Function | Line range | Approx LOC | Difficulty | Why |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
| `seed_rng` | 20 | 1 | trivial | Single libc `srand()` → `np.random.RandomState(seed)`. |
| `get_initiations` | 27 | ~20 | easy | Plain counting loop. |
| `get_codon_at` | 48 | ~20 | easy | Index lookup with bounds check. |
| `get_candidates_to_C` | 68 | ~28 | easy | Forward scan over a sequence. |
| `get_candidates_to_N` | 96 | ~28 | easy | Mirror of `_to_C` going N-ward. |
| `select_candidate` | 124 | ~25 | medium | Uses `rand()` — first place the RNG seam shows up. |
| `is_initial_state` | 149 | ~15 | easy | `cdef bint` predicate. |
| **`reconcile_via_ribosome_positions`** | 164 | **~186** | hard | Per-ribosome codon-step inner kernel. |
| **`reconcile_via_trna_pools`** | 350 | **~114** | hard | Pool-balance accounting with stochastic rounding. |
| **`get_elongation_rate`** | 464 | **~159** | hard | The numerical solver for the per-tick rate. |
| `get_codons_read` | 623 | ~15 | easy | Post-step aggregation. |

That's **~120 lines of easy work** + **3 hard kernels** (~460 lines combined). Each function has a clean signature, can be parity-tested in isolation against the upstream Cython output, and lands as its own commit.

## Proposed task split

Replace the single Task #2 with these:

| New task | Subject | Estimate |
|---|---|---|
| 2a | Set up parity-test scaffold: build upstream Cython kernel once, dump golden inputs/outputs for representative seeds + states into `tests/fixtures/trna_charging_kernel_golden.json.gz`. Create empty `v2ecoli/processes/polypeptide/kinetic_charging_kernel.py` with module-level numba RNG wrapper. | 1 session, ~2–3 hr |
| 2b | Port the 8 easy functions (`seed_rng`, `get_initiations`, `get_codon_at`, `get_candidates_to_C`, `get_candidates_to_N`, `select_candidate`, `is_initial_state`, `get_codons_read`). Parity test against golden. | 1 session, ~2–3 hr |
| 2c | Port `reconcile_via_ribosome_positions`. Parity test. | 1 session, ~3–4 hr |
| 2d | Port `reconcile_via_trna_pools`. Parity test. | 1 session, ~3–4 hr |
| 2e | Port `get_elongation_rate`. Parity test. Port the upstream 580-line `test_trna_charging.py` into `tests/test_trna_charging_kernel.py` (most assertions wire straight to the new module). | 1 session, ~3–4 hr |

So **5 sessions of 2–4 hours each**, instead of "1–2 days." Each session commits independently and is debug-isolated — if `reconcile_via_trna_pools` has a parity gap, sessions 2a, 2b, 2c, 2e still ship clean.

The 2a scaffold session is the most important one to do first because it lets every later session prove correctness mechanically (golden inputs → expected outputs) rather than by inspection. Without it you'd be trusting that the port "looks right."

**The split, now in the task list + HANDOFF (committed `5c26f0f`):**

| Sub-task | Subject | Sizing | Blocked by |
|---|---|---|---|
| #14 = 2a | Parity-test scaffold + golden capture + RNG wrapper | 2–3 hr | — |
| #15 = 2b | 8 easy functions (~120 LOC) | 2–3 hr | 2a |
| #16 = 2c | `reconcile_via_ribosome_positions` (~186 LOC) | 3–4 hr | 2a |
| #17 = 2d | `reconcile_via_trna_pools` (~114 LOC) | 3–4 hr | 2a |
| #18 = 2e | `get_elongation_rate` (~159 LOC) + 580-line companion test port | 3–4 hr | 2c, 2d |

## [2026-06-09T15:07:26.526Z] assistant

Two observations that shape Task #2a:

1. The upstream test file already hardcodes input+expected output for every kernel function — I can read the assertions directly, no Cython build needed for deterministic cases.
2. Stochastic functions (`select_candidate`, `reconcile_via_*`) use libc `rand()`. macOS and Linux `rand()` differ, and our numpy/numba port will use a *different* RNG entirely. So byte-for-byte parity isn't realistic; the design needs to handle this.

Let me check if I can actually build the upstream Cython kernel to capture macOS-libc-rand() outputs, and also look at what the upstream build does.

---

## [2026-06-09T15:29:15.519Z] assistant

Task 2a complete. Two commits on `trna_charging_final`:
- `7bbea48` — scaffold (golden + RNG wrapper + stub module + 10-test gate)
- `ee9c91a` — HANDOFF pointer bump

## What landed

| Artifact | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `tests/fixtures/trna_charging_kernel_golden.json.gz` (1.7 KB) | 25 cases × 9 functions, captured from the upstream Cython kernel after building it in `vEcoli_trna/.venv` |
| `v2ecoli/processes/polypeptide/kinetic_charging_kernel.py` | `seed()` / `randint_below()` RNG wrapper backed by `numpy.random.RandomState` (independent of the global numpy.random state) + 10 NotImplementedError stubs |
| `workspace/investigations/trna-charging-final/capture_kernel_golden.py` | Re-runnable script — re-captures the golden if upstream HEAD changes |
| `tests/test_kinetic_charging_kernel_scaffold.py` | 10-test gate — all green |

**Verified:**
- `pytest tests/test_kinetic_charging_kernel_scaffold.py` → 10 passed in 5.0 s.
- Upstream Cython kernel built fine and `pytest wholecell/tests/utils/test_trna_charging.py` → 15 passed in 0.44 s. Confirms our captures came from a kernel that itself agrees with its own test suite.

## Important design call I had to make

The kernel calls `libc rand()`. macOS `rand()` ≠ glibc `rand()` ≠ `numpy.random.RandomState.randint()`. So **byte-for-byte parity vs the upstream Cython is impossible by design** unless we link to libc via ctypes (brittle, not worth it).

Policy: deterministic functions (8 of 11) use the captured libc-rand golden directly — they don't touch the RNG, so exact-equality holds. Stochastic functions (3) get a sibling `numpy-randomstate.json.gz` golden captured at port time in 2c/2d, against which they assert byte-identity. The libc-rand golden then exists only as a sanity-shape check for the stochastic functions.

This is documented in the module docstring, the golden's metadata note, and the audit's "Task #2a progress log" section.

**Next session:** Task #2b (8 easy kernel functions). The scaffold's `kernel.seed` / `kernel.randint_below` is ready; `select_candidate` is the first place the RNG seam gets exercised, so it's also the first sanity check that the wrapper behaves under real use.

## [2026-06-09T15:46:38.172Z] assistant

Task 2b complete. Two commits on `trna_charging_final`:
- `4ab2763` — 7 deterministic kernel functions + parity test
- `e8617e5` — HANDOFF pointer bump

## What landed

All 7 deterministic functions in `v2ecoli/processes/polypeptide/kinetic_charging_kernel.py`, each `@njit(error_model="numpy")` to match the precedent in `polypeptide/kinetics.py`:

| Function | Notes |
|---|---|
| `get_initiations` | Plain count. `indexes` arg kept for API parity but unused. |
| `get_codon_at` | Bounded lookup; returns −1 outside range. Called from all the others. |
| `get_candidates_to_C` / `_N` | Directional scans. Cleaner signature than upstream (dropped 4 scratch params). |
| `select_candidate` | **Discovery**: purely deterministic. Upstream's `r = rand() % candidates` happens at the *caller*, not inside `select_candidate` — so the RNG seam is first exercised in 2c, not here. |
| `is_initial_state` | Upstream Cython flags it as unused at build (compiler warning). Ported anyway for API symmetry. |
| `get_codons_read` | Codon-usage histogram. |

## Parity verification

`tests/test_kinetic_charging_kernel.py` (new) — 8 tests, one per function plus a coverage gate. Every relevant case from `tests/fixtures/trna_charging_kernel_golden.json.gz` is loaded and asserted bit-identical vs the upstream Cython output.

```
pytest tests/test_kinetic_charging_kernel{,_scaffold}.py
  -> 18 passed, 3 skipped, 3.93s (incl. numba JIT warm-up)
```

The 3 skipped are 2c/2d/2e's still-stubbed functions; their test bodies are placeholders awaiting implementation.

**Next session:** Task #2c (`reconcile_via_ribosome_positions`, ~186 LOC). First place the RNG seam is exercised inside the kernel itself — upstream's `rand() % candidates` becomes `kernel.randint_below(candidates)`. A sibling `numpy-randomstate.json.gz` golden gets captured at port time and parity becomes byte-identical against *that*, with the existing libc-rand golden kept only for shape sanity (verifying sums of mutated arrays match).

## [2026-06-09T15:52:02.117Z] assistant

Two design calls I'm making explicit before writing code:

1. **`disagreements_remaining` state leak is intentional.** Initialized to True at function entry; phase 1 sets it False on exit and never resets; phase 2 resets it to True before its own loop. So on attempt 2+, phase 1 is skipped entirely. The `test_reconcile_attempts_threshold` expected output `[10, 15]` depends on this. My port preserves it.

2. **Plain Python orchestration, not `@njit` on the outer.** The function needs to call `kernel.randint_below` (Python-side `RandomState`). Numba's `@njit` would conflict with that. The hot work is already in jit'd 2b helpers (`get_codon_at`, `get_candidates_to_C/N`, `select_candidate`); the outer 4 attempts × short while loops add negligible overhead.

## [2026-06-09T15:58:43.558Z] assistant

Task 2c done. Two commits:
- `c602c64` — port + capture script + per-RNG golden + tests
- `cc6b0f0` — HANDOFF pointer bump

## What landed

**Implementation (`v2ecoli/processes/polypeptide/kinetic_charging_kernel.py`):**
- ~140 LOC pure-Python orchestration of the two-phase reconciliation loop. Calls 2b's `@njit` helpers (`get_codon_at`, `get_candidates_to_C/N`, `select_candidate`) for the hot work; RNG draws go through `kernel.randint_below`.

**Two non-obvious upstream behaviors I had to preserve:**
1. `disagreements_remaining` leaks across attempts — it's initialized once at function entry, set False by phase 1 and never reset. So on attempt 2+, phase 1 is *skipped*. `test_reconcile_attempts_threshold`'s `[10, 15]` expected output depends on this; without the leak the algorithm would oscillate forever on non-convergent inputs.
2. Phase 2 has no `exhausted` array — a surplus codon means it must exist somewhere in the consumed range, so candidates ≥ 1 is guaranteed.

**Parity (two complementary gates):**
- Byte-identity vs `tests/fixtures/trna_charging_kernel_numpy_randomstate_golden.json.gz` — committed regression catcher.
- Algorithmic invariants vs the libc-rand golden: `kinetics_codons` immutable, non-negativity, conservation (`Δelongations.sum == Δsequence_codons.sum`), convergence parity (if upstream reached compromise=0, v2ecoli does too).

**Sanity:** For `equal` (0 RNG calls): byte-identical libc ↔ numpy. For `attempts_threshold`: both reach `sequence_codons=[10,15]` and `elongations.sum()=25` despite different per-ribosome trajectories. Confirms the algorithm's convergence is RNG-invariant.

**Results:** `pytest tests/test_kinetic_charging_kernel{,_scaffold}.py` → **20 passed, 2 skipped** in 3.78 s.

**Next session:** Task #2d (`reconcile_via_trna_pools`, ~114 LOC). Same shape as 2c — Python orchestration, 3 RNG draws per inner iteration (codon, free-vs-charged tRNA, ribosome). After landing, re-run `capture_numpy_randomstate_golden.py` to refresh the golden (the existing one has the 2 cases waiting as `skipped`). Invariants for the libc-shape gate: free+charged tRNA conservation, chargings never negative, amino_acids_used parallel to chargings.

394			# Identify free tRNA candidates

397				if trnas_to_codons[codon, i] == 1:
398					candidates += free_trnas[i]

## [2026-06-09T16:08:16.095Z] assistant

Task 2d done. Two commits:
- `dfed1f4` — port + golden refresh + 2 new parity tests
- `4442e93` — HANDOFF pointer bump

## What landed

**Implementation:** ~95 LOC of pure-Python orchestration calling `randint_below` for the codon and tRNA picks. Two-branch structure:

| Branch | Trigger | Net effect |
|---|---|---|
| Free-tRNA | ≥1 free tRNA reads the surplus codon | `free_trnas[i]−=1`, `charged_trnas[i]+=1`, counter−=1, kinetics−=1 |
| Charged-tRNA | No free tRNAs | `chargings[i]−=1`, `amino_acids_used[aa]−=1`, counter−=1, kinetics−=1. Free/charged unchanged (the tRNA goes free → charged → free in a single accounting step) |

**Key difference from 2c:** this function **mutates `kinetics_codons`** (one decrement per pick). `sequence_codons` is the read-only input.

**Parity (same two-gate pattern as 2c):**
- Byte-identity vs numpy-RandomState golden — refreshed to 10 cases (was 8, the 2 trna_pools placeholders now filled).
- Algorithmic invariants vs libc-rand golden: `sequence_codons` immutable, per-tRNA conservation (`free+charged` unchanged), post-loop `kinetics_codons ≤ sequence_codons`, non-negativity on `chargings`/`amino_acids_used`/counter, plus **`kinetics_codons` final state is RNG-invariant** (asserted byte-identical vs upstream — because the loop runs until disagreements=0 with one decrement per iteration, the total decrement is fully determined).

**Sanity:** Both test cases yield byte-identical libc ↔ numpy outputs because the inputs only allow one viable tRNA pick per iteration.

**Results:** `pytest tests/test_kinetic_charging_kernel{,_scaffold}.py` → **22 passed, 1 skipped** in 3.83 s. The remaining skip is `get_elongation_rate` for 2e.

**Next session:** Task #2e (`get_elongation_rate`, ~159 LOC). Pure deterministic — no RNG, no mutating writes. Binary search over `col` for the elongation rate matching a target. Can use `@njit` directly (no Python orchestration needed). Plus the 580-line upstream companion test to port.

## [2026-06-09T16:20:48.558Z] assistant

Task 2e complete. **Task #2 (the full Cython kernel port) is done.** Two commits:
- `60474af` — `get_elongation_rate` implementation + parity test + stub-gate
- `44b334a` — HANDOFF pointer bump

## 2e summary

**Implementation:** ~110 LOC `@njit` binary search. Pure deterministic — no RNG, no mutating inputs.

**Two upstream quirks preserved verbatim for byte-identity:**
- `best_col % 2 == 0` heuristic is only strictly correct for `time_int ≤ 2`; matched anyway.
- When `rate == target` breaks early, the "find best" sweep `for i in range(index)` skips the just-written entry. Upstream's Cython works around this with `cdef int i = 0`; my port initializes `best_i = 0` to match.

**Parity:**
- `test_get_elongation_rate_parity`: bit-identical vs upstream Cython. ✓
- `test_reconcile_seed_propagates_to_kernel_output`: surprising finding while writing this — the big_seed_12345 vs big_seed_54321 inputs converge to *identical* state under `numpy.random.RandomState` (libc rand gave different elongations on the same inputs). Real RNG × inputs coincidence, not a port bug. Adapted to use `attempts_threshold` inputs (10 ribosomes, 5 viable forward picks) where divergence is reliable. ✓

**Stub gate** (`test_no_stub_functions_remain`): iterates every documented kernel function with shape-minimal inputs and fails fast if any still raises `NotImplementedError`.

## Task #2 grand summary

Upstream's 638-LOC Cython `_trna_charging.pyx` (11 functions) is now `v2ecoli/processes/polypeptide/kinetic_charging_kernel.py` (~700 LOC Python + numba). Two committed goldens enforce parity:

| Golden | Cases | Function coverage |
|---|---:|---|
| `trna_charging_kernel_golden.json.gz` (libc-rand) | 25 | All 9 callable functions |
| `trna_charging_kernel_numpy_randomstate_golden.json.gz` | 10 | The 2 stochastic functions |

8 deterministic functions → byte-identity vs upstream. 3 stochastic → byte-identity vs per-RNG numpy golden + algorithmic invariants (conservation, non-negativity, convergence) vs upstream libc golden.

**Results:** 24 passed, 0 skipped in 2.89s under `-m 'not sim'`.

**Next session:** Task #3 (`KineticTrnaChargingModel` class + `kinetic_charging_baseline` composite arch + behavior test). The kernel is ready to consume.

## [2026-06-09T16:27:46.627Z] assistant

Task #3 is genuinely 3–5 sessions of work — the upstream `KineticTrnaChargingModel` is 710 lines, and v2ecoli's polypeptide architecture is different (each elongation model is its own subclass of `BasePolypeptideElongation`, not a strategy on a single `PolypeptideElongation` process). I'd like to split it the same way as Task #2 before diving in.

## Proposed split

| Sub-task | Subject | Est. time |
|---|---|---|
| 3a | Class scaffold: new `v2ecoli/processes/polypeptide/kinetic_charging.py` with `KineticTrnaChargingPolypeptideElongation` extending `BasePolypeptideElongation`. Full `config_schema` matching upstream's parameter surface. All methods stubbed with `NotImplementedError` markers. Smoke test that the class imports and `config_schema` is well-formed. | 1.5–2 hr |
| 3b | Port `__init__` parameter unpacking (~80 LOC upstream). Tile-up of constants, slice indexes, mapping arrays, kinetic parameters. Smoke-test instantiation from a hand-built parameters dict. | 1.5–2 hr |
| 3c | Port `elongation_rate()` + `request()` (~120 LOC). The early-tick methods. Uses our `get_elongation_rate` kernel + `buildSequences`. | 2 hr |
| 3d | Port `evolve_state()` (~250 LOC, the heart). Uses `reconcile_via_ribosome_positions` and `reconcile_via_trna_pools`. The most subtle method. | 3–4 hr |
| 3e | Port `monomer_limit`, `isTimeStepShortEnough`, listener emission, any remaining methods (~100 LOC). | 1.5 hr |
| 3f | Composite arch (`v2ecoli/composites/kinetic_charging_baseline.py`) via `@composite_generator`, `__init__.py` and `cache_version.py` updates, behavior test `tests/test_behavior_kinetic_charging.py`. **Depends on Task #5** (library/sim_data deltas) to actually populate the new parameters from `sim_data` — without that the behavior test can't run end-to-end. | 2 hr (port) + blocked on #5 (test) |

## A wrinkle

Task #3 has a real dependency on Task #5: the kinetic-charging parameters live in `sim_data` (populated by ParCa via the Relation dataclass we ported in 885df04), but `v2ecoli/library/sim_data.py` needs to be extended to extract them and pass them to the process's `config_schema` defaults. Without that, 3f's behavior test will fail at instantiation.

Options for handling this:
1. **Defer 3f's behavior test** — do 3a–3f without the end-to-end test, then revisit after Task #5.
2. **Interleave**: do Task #5 between 3e and 3f.
3. **Mock the params**: build the behavior test with a hand-stitched params dict so it doesn't need `sim_data` plumbing. Risky — the mocks drift from production.

695	    def elongation_rate(self, states):
696	        """Media-set ribosome elongation rate (Base/Supply model).
697	
698	        Governing relation::
699	
700	            v_elong = min(basal_elongation_rate,
701	                          elngRateFactor * rate(media_id))   [aa/s]
702	
703	        Looks up the per-media basal rate and scales it by ``elngRateFactor``,
704	        then caps it at the model's ``basal_elongation_rate`` (~22 aa/s).
705	        """
706	        current_media_id = states["environment"]["media_id"]
707	        rate = (
708	            self.elngRateFactor
709	            * self.ribosomeElongationRateDict[current_media_id]
710	            .to(units.aa / units.s).magnitude
711	        )
712	        return np.min([self.basal_elongation_rate, rate])

830	class SteadyStatePolypeptideElongation(TranslationSupplyPolypeptideElongation):
831	    """Steady-state tRNA-charging elongation model (Michaelis-Menten competitive
832	    inhibition). This is the growth-rate-control model: the ribosome elongation
833	    rate emerges from amino-acid availability rather than being imposed.
834	
835	    It composes three growth-rate-control modules, each a focused unit:
836	
837	    * **tRNA charging** — the steady-state charged-fraction / elongation-rate
838	      solve (``kinetics.calculate_trna_charging``), plus the per-tRNA
839	      distribution bookkeeping (``distribution_from_aa``) applied in
840	      ``request`` (charge requests) and ``evolve`` (realized charging reactions).
841	    * **ppGpp (RelA/SpoT)** — ``elongation_rate`` (ppGpp-modulated rate),
842	      ``_ppgpp_request`` (predicted ppGpp turnover → bulk requests) and
843	      ``_ppgpp_evolve`` (realized ppGpp synthesis/degradation), over
844	      ``kinetics.ppgpp_metabolite_changes``.
845	    * **amino-acid supply** — ``_amino_acid_supply`` (synthesis / import / export
846	      rates + the supply closure the charging solve integrates).
847	
848	    ``request`` and ``evolve`` orchestrate these three in sequence per the
849	    partitioned request→allocate→evolve contract.
850	    """

13	Each active ribosome extends its polypeptide by incorporating amino acids
14	from the template sequence. The ``polymerize`` algorithm distributes
15	limited amino acid pools across all ribosomes to maximize total elongation:
16	
17	    sequences = buildSequences(protein_seqs, ribosome_positions, v * dt)
18	    result = polymerize(sequences, aa_counts, rate_limit)
19	
20	Each amino acid incorporation also consumes GTP for EF-Tu/EF-G cycling:
21	
22	    GTP_consumed = n_elongations * gtpPerElongation  (default 4.2 GTP/aa)
23	
24	**ppGpp regulation** (optional, via ``include_ppgpp``)
25	
26	ppGpp inhibits elongation rate through a fitted relationship:
27	
28	    v_elong = f_ppgpp([ppGpp])   [aa/s]
29	
30	ppGpp synthesis/degradation is modeled via RelA and SpoT enzymes:
31	
32	    d[ppGpp]/dt = k_RelA * [RelA] * [uncharged_tRNA] / (KD + [uncharged_tRNA])
33	                - k_SpoT * [SpoT] * [ppGpp] / (KI + [ppGpp])
34	                + k_SpoT_syn * [SpoT]
35	
36	**tRNA charging** (modelled by ``SteadyStatePolypeptideElongation``)
37	
38	For each amino acid species a, the fraction of charged tRNA is tracked:
39	
40	    f_charged_a = [charged_tRNA_a] / ([charged_tRNA_a] + [uncharged_tRNA_a])
41	
42	The effective elongation rate is modulated by the minimum f_charged across
43	all amino acid species, coupling translation speed to tRNA availability.
44	
45	Charging reactions consume amino acids, ATP, and uncharged tRNAs, producing
46	charged tRNAs, AMP, and PPi, governed by aminoacyl-tRNA synthetase kinetics.
47	
48	**Amino acid supply** (optional, via ``mechanistic_aa_transport``)
49	
50	Amino acid pools are replenished by synthesis, import, and recycled from
51	degradation. Exp
…[truncated 1581 chars]

Task 3a done. Two commits:
- `07f7225` — class scaffold + 20 smoke tests
- `379cfe1` — HANDOFF pointer bump

## What landed

**New `v2ecoli/processes/polypeptide/kinetic_charging.py`:**
- `KineticTrnaChargingPolypeptideElongation` extending `BasePolypeptideElongation` (peer of `SteadyStatePolypeptideElongation`, matching v2ecoli's one-Process-per-model convention vs upstream's strategy pattern).
- `config_schema = {**Base.config_schema, ...12 new kinetic keys...}`. New keys cover codon-sequence tables, tRNA↔codon mapping, kinetic constants, and reconciliation buffer. Defaults are empty/zero-shaped — Task #5 wires them up from `sim_data.relation`.
- 14 method stubs, each raising `NotImplementedError("…Task 3b"/"3c"/"3d"/"3e")` so any accidental composite build against the partial port tells you which session to pick up.

**Architectural decision documented:** no circular import because `polypeptide_elongation.py` doesn't import the new file — 3f's composite arch wrapper is the only consumer.

**Smoke test (`tests/test_kinetic_charging_polypeptide_elongation_scaffold.py`):**
- 6 structural checks (imports, subclass relationship, name/topology inheritance, all 12 kinetic keys present, base keys preserved, well-formed schema entries).
- 14 parametric checks — one per stub — verifying the task-marker string in each stub's source. Catches drift if 3b lands `initialize` but forgets to drop the marker elsewhere.

**Results:** 20 passed, 1 warning, 3.80s.

Task #3 split (now in HANDOFF):

| Sub-task | Status |
|---|:-:|
| 3a class scaffold | ✅ |
| 3b `__init__` parameter unpacking | next |
| 3c `elongation_rate` + `request` + helpers | |
| 3d `evolve` (heart) | |
| 3e `monomer_limit` + timestep + listeners | |
| ⏯ Task #5 (sim_data plumbing) — interleaved before 3f | |
| 3f composite arch + behavior test | blocked on #5 |

**Next session:** Task 3b — port the upstream `KineticTrnaChargingModel.__init__` (~80 LOC). All 12 config_schema keys are already in place; 3b unpacks them into instance attrs, builds the slice indexes, derives `trnas_to_amino_acid_indexes`, sets `previous_rate`. Dropping the `Task 3b` markers from `initialize` and `get_kinetic_constants` is the signal that 3b is complete.

import os
import pytest

CACHE = "out/cache"
# Cache+run() tests are `sim` (CI routes them to the behavior-tests job that
# has the cache; the unit job runs `-m "not slow and not sim"`). The pure
# import/subclass test below needs no cache and stays in the fast unit job, so
# there is no module-level pytestmark — gating is per-test via `_needs_cache`.
_needs_cache = pytest.mark.skipif(
    not os.path.isdir(CACHE) and not os.environ.get("CI"),
    reason=f"cache dir {CACHE!r} not present",
)


def test_three_variants_importable_and_subclassed():
    from v2ecoli.steps.partition import PartitionedProcess
    from v2ecoli.processes.polypeptide_elongation import (
        BasePolypeptideElongation,
        TranslationSupplyPolypeptideElongation,
        SteadyStatePolypeptideElongation,
    )
    assert issubclass(BasePolypeptideElongation, PartitionedProcess)
    assert issubclass(TranslationSupplyPolypeptideElongation, BasePolypeptideElongation)
    assert issubclass(SteadyStatePolypeptideElongation,
                      TranslationSupplyPolypeptideElongation)

## [2026-06-09T17:07:50.118Z] assistant

Task 3b done. Two commits:
- `9e62c27` — `initialize` + `get_kinetic_constants` + 5 new tests
- `169fb68` — HANDOFF pointer bump

## What landed

**`initialize(config)`** (~90 LOC) — calls `super().initialize(config)` then unpacks all 12 kinetic-charging keys into instance attrs:
- Constants, codon sequences, slice indexes for the 6-segment molecules buffer.
- Mapping arrays (`trnas_to_amino_acids/codons`, `codons_to_amino_acids`) + the derived `trnas_to_amino_acid_indexes` (per-tRNA aa index in `np.int8` for kernel memory footprint).
- `max_attempts = np.byte(4)`, `buffer = reconciliation_buffer`.
- Kinetic params `k_cat__per_s`, `K_M_amino_acid__per_L`, `K_M_trna__per_L`.
- `previous_rate` warm-start for next tick's binary search.

**`get_kinetic_constants(cell_mass)`** (~5 LOC) — converts per-litre Michaelis constants to per-cell using current volume.

**Upstream-vs-v2ecoli differences documented in docstrings:**
- `self.process.X` → `self.X` (v2ecoli's class IS the process).
- `cellDensity` matches base schema key (upstream uses `cell_density`).
- `n_avogadro` set by base; not re-fetched.
- pint Quantities (`.to(units.L).magnitude`) instead of Unum's `.asNumber()`.

**Tests:** 20 → 23 scaffold tests:
- Drops `initialize` / `get_kinetic_constants` from the still-stubbed marker list.
- `test_initialize_is_no_longer_a_stub` — fails loud on regression.
- `test_initialize_sets_documented_attrs_via_source_scan` — 27 expected `self.X = …` assignments must appear (pure source scan, no cache needed).
- `test_get_kinetic_constants_uses_cell_volume_conversion` — source scan.
- `@pytest.mark.sim @_needs_cache test_initialize_runs_end_to_end_against_cache` — full instantiation: loads real cache, augments with synthetic kinetic extensions via new `_make_kinetic_extensions` helper, verifies every attr lands.
- `@pytest.mark.sim @_needs_cache test_get_kinetic_constants_returns_volume_scaled_arrays` — doubling cell_mass doubles K_M arrays.

**Results:** 23 passed (scaffold), 47 passed (all kinetic-charging tests) in 3.3s. No regressions.

**Bonus for 3c–3e:** `_make_kinetic_extensions(n_aas, n_trnas, n_codons, n_proteins, n_synthetases)` helper is exported in the test module — the next sub-tasks reuse it instead of re-writing the synthetic-config boilerplate.

**Next session:** Task #3c — `elongation_rate`, `request`, `run_model`, `codon_sequences_width`, `sequences`, `max_charging_rate`. Watch for the signature mismatch I flagged in 3a's HANDOFF: v2ecoli's `request(states, aasInSequences)` doesn't get the `protein_indexes`/`peptide_lengths` upstream's `request` expects — re-derive from `states["active_ribosome"]` in the method (cheap).

## [2026-06-09T20:12:13.340Z] assistant

Task 3c done. Two commits:
- `b2b1477` — request-side methods + 5 new scaffold tests
- `930237b` — HANDOFF pointer bump

## What landed

**~430 LOC across 6 methods + 1 base override:**

| Method | LOC | Purpose |
|---|---:|---|
| `_init_bulk_indices` (override) | 10 | Adds `atp_idx`, `amp_idx`, `ppi_idx`, `met_idx`, `map_idx` to the base layout |
| `elongation_rate(states)` | 45 | Builds `self.longer_sequences`; calls `kernel.get_elongation_rate`; warm-starts `previous_rate` |
| `request(states, aasInSequences)` | 95 | Runs `run_model("bulk_total")`; emits bulk requests for AAs/ATP/tRNAs/synthetases/MAP/water |
| `run_model(codons, attr, states)` | 270 | `scipy.solve_ivp` RK45 ODE with sin/sin² roll-offs, AA/tRNA reconciliation, listener emission |
| `max_charging_rate(states, attr)` | 5 | `v_max = k_cat * n_synthetases` |
| `codon_sequences_width(elongation_rates)` | 1 | Returns `self.sequences_width` |
| `sequences(sequences)` | 1 | Returns `self.longer_sequences` (arg ignored, API parity) |

**Upstream-vs-v2ecoli diffs documented in each docstring:**
- `self.process.X_idx` → `self.X_idx` (v2ecoli's class IS the process).
- Unum `.asNumber(...)` → pint `.to(...).magnitude`.
- v2ecoli's `request(states, aasInSequences)` contract — `aasInSequences` is amino-acid-domain and intentionally ignored; the kinetic model recomputes its own codon-domain `monomers_in_sequences` from `self.longer_sequences`.

**Tests:** 23 → 28 scaffold tests:
- Drops 6 entries from the still-stubbed marker list.
- `test_3c_method_no_longer_stub` (parametric, 7 methods incl. `_init_bulk_indices`).
- `test_elongation_rate_calls_kernel_and_sets_longer_sequences` — source-scan.
- `test_request_requests_all_kinetic_bulk_keys` — verifies all 7 bulk indices + return tuple.
- `test_run_model_uses_ode_and_kernel_constants` — verifies `solve_ivp`/RK45/tolerances/K_M caches/stochasticRound/7-tuple return.
- `test_init_bulk_indices_adds_kinetic_keys` — verifies `super()` call + 5 new indices.

**Why source-scan instead of runtime:** `run_model` end-to-end needs both the cache populated with kinetic config keys (synthetic-shape-only today via `_make_kinetic_extensions`) AND the `active_ribosome`/`bulk`/`listeners` state plausibly initialized. End-to-end comes together in Task 3f's behavior test after Task #5 wires sim_data.

**Results:** `pytest tests/test_kinetic_charging_polypeptide_elongation_scaffold.py` → 28 passed in 4.74 s. `pytest tests/test_kinetic_charging_*.py -m 'not sim'` → 50 passed in 3.52 s. No regressions.

**Next session:** Task #3d — `evolve`, `reconcile`, `protein_maturation`, `final_amino_acids`. The heart of the model. `reconcile` uses the kernel's `reconcile_via_*` pair. Watch for the signature mismatch — v2ecoli's `evolve(states, total_aa_counts, aas_used, next_amino_acid_count, nElongations, nInitialized)` differs from upstream's; the kinetic model probably needs to override `evolve_state` directly rather than just the `evolve` helper.

## [2026-06-09T20:32:10.939Z] assistant

Task 3d done; 3e collapsed into it. Two commits:
- `4033505` — `evolve_state` override + 7 evolve-side methods + 8 new scaffold tests
- `9867cca` — HANDOFF pointer bump

## Scope expansion (flagged upfront)

v2ecoli's `BasePolypeptideElongation.evolve_state` is amino-acid-centric — it polymerizes against `aa_counts_for_translation`. The kinetic model needs codon-based polymerize → reconcile → protein_maturation → evolve, so I overrode `evolve_state` entirely (~225 LOC). That override consumes `monomer_to_aa`, `monomer_limit`, `next_amino_acids` — slated for 3e — so I pulled them forward. **3e is now collapsed into 3d.**

## What landed (~450 LOC across 7 method bodies + 1 override)

| Method | LOC | Purpose |
|---|---:|---|
| `evolve_state` (override) | 225 | Codon-based replacement for the base; builds AA + codon sequences, polymerizes, runs reconcile + protein_maturation + evolve, emits listeners |
| `reconcile` | 80 | Runs `run_model("bulk")`; on disagreement seeds `kernel.seed` and dispatches the 2c/2d kernel pair |
| `protein_maturation` | 65 | MAP kinetics (k_cat=6/s) cap cleavages; multinomial roll-back for over-demand |
| `evolve` (9-arg) | 55 | Bulk deltas: water/tRNAs/AAs/ATP/AMP/PPi/proton/Met |
| `monomer_to_aa` | 1 | `codons_to_amino_acids @ monomer` |
| `monomer_limit` | 4 | Returns the codon-domain prediction tuple |
| `next_amino_acids` | 1 | Returns 0 (Base default) |
| `final_amino_acids` | — | Stays as `NotImplementedError` with a bypass-explanation message |

## Tests

`tests/test_kinetic_charging_polypeptide_elongation_scaffold.py` 28 → 31:
- Drops the parametric `test_method_stub_carries_task_marker`.
- New `test_no_task_3_stub_markers_remain_in_any_method` — sentinel that scans every method's source for `raise NotImplementedError` paired with a `Task 3X` marker (docstring cross-refs ignored).
- 8 new source-scan tests covering `evolve_state` override hooks, both kernel reconcile dispatches, MAP kinetics, all 9 bulk indices in `evolve`, the `final_amino_acids` bypass message, and the three pulled-forward helpers.

**Results:** `pytest tests/test_kinetic_charging_polypeptide_elongation_scaffold.py` → 31 passed in 2.85 s. `pytest tests/test_kinetic_charging_*.py -m 'not sim'` → 53 passed in 2.99 s. No regressions.

## Updated remaining plan

| Session | Task | Status |
|---|---|---|
| ~~9–12~~ | 3a–3d | ✅ |
| 13 (next) | Task #5 — library/sim_data deltas | unblocks 3f |
| 14 | Task #3f — composite arch + behavior test | depends on #5 |
| 15 | Task #4 — other process deltas | independent |
| 16 | Task #8 — full ParCa rerun | mostly compute |
| 17 | Tasks #9–#13 — cache rebuild + tests + parity + reports | |

**Next session:** Task #5 — populate the new `config_schema` keys (`codon_sequences`, `residue_weights_by_codon`, `trnas_to_codons`, `k_cat__per_s`, etc.) from `sim_data.relation` in v2ecoli's config-build path. The Relation dataclass and its `_build_trna_charging_kinetics` method are already in place from Task #6 (commit `885df04`); 3f's behavior test waits on this.

- `f523058` — HANDOFF pointer bump

## What landed

**Scope kept narrow:** the audit listed 5 upstream library files (+289 LOC combined). Only the `sim_data.py` deltas affect 3f. The other four are upstream refactor noise / already covered by recent v2ecoli PRs — evaluated and deferred (each documented in audit).

**`LoadSimData._kinetic_charging_extensions(relation) → dict`** in `v2ecoli/library/sim_data.py` — returns the 12 keys consumed by `KineticTrnaChargingPolypeptideElongation`:

| Source | Key |
|---|---|
| `relation.codon_sequences` etc. | codon_sequences, residue_weights_by_codon, n_codons, i_start_codon |
| `translation.monomer_data["cleavage_of_initial_methionine"]` (added in Task #6) | is_map_substrate |
| `relation.trna_codon_pairs`, `relation.trnas_to_codons`, `relation.codons_to_amino_acids` | n_trna_codon_pairs, trnas_to_codons, codons_to_amino_acids |
| `relation.synthetase_to_k_cat / _K_A`, `relation.trna_to_K_T` (Unum → pint, Avogadro-scaled) | k_cat__per_s, K_M_amino_acid__per_L, K_M_trna__per_L |
| `relation.reconciliation_buffer` | reconciliation_buffer |

The dict is splatted at the end of the existing `polypeptide_elongation_config` dict via `**kinetic_charging_config`. Steady-state ignores; kinetic consumes.

**Soft-fail path:** returns `{}` when `sim_data.relation` is missing or the 145-line pre-port stub (no `codon_sequences` attr). Keeps the existing steady-state path working against the current pre-port cache; composites selecting the kinetic process will fail loud when their `config_schema` reads come up empty/zero-shaped.

**Unum → pint:** documented in the docstring. `unum_to_pint(...).to(...).magnitude` for each kinetic param. Defaults match upstream (selenocysteine-canonical `1e4/s`, `1 µmol/L`).

## Tests

`tests/test_kinetic_charging_sim_data_config.py` — 8 tests:
- 2 soft-fail tests (`None` / pre-port stub).
- 4 mock-based behavior tests (all 12 keys, dtypes, k_cat in 1/s, K_M Avogadro-scaling).
- 2 source-scan tests (extension is splatted; docstring documents the Unum boundary).

**Results:** 8 passed in 3.91 s. Full kinetic-charging + polypeptide fast tests: 62 passed in 3.69 s. Scaffold tests including cache-gated 3b: 31 passed (exercising the soft-fail path against the stale cache).

## What unblocks

- **3f's composite arch can land now** — the config keys exist.
- **3f's end-to-end behavior test waits on Task #8** (full ParCa rerun) — only then does `sim_data.relation` carry the post-port attrs and the extension method returns the full kinetic dict.

## Remaining plan

| Session | Task | Status |
|---|---|---|
| ~~13~~ | Task #5 — library/sim_data | ✅ |
| 14 (next) | Task #3f — composite arch + behavior test scaffold | unblocks Task #8 testing |
| 15 | Task #4 — other process deltas | independent |
| 16 | Task #8 — full ParCa rerun (compute) | enables behavior tests |
| 17 | Tasks #9–#13 — cache rebuild + tests + parity + reports | |

**Next session:** Task #3f — `v2ecoli/composites/kinetic_charging_baseline.py` with `@composite_generator`, register in `composites/__init__.py`, add to `cache_version.INPUT_FILES`, plus `tests/test_behavior_kinetic_charging.py` (skipped until Task #8).

28	def _derive_process_seed(master_seed: int, process_name: str) -> int:
29	    """Derive a per-process RNG seed from (master_seed, process_name).
30	
31	    Without this, all stochastic processes inherit the same cache-derived seed,
32	    so multi-seed ensembles collapse to bit-identical trajectories. CRC32 with
33	    master_seed as the initial state, keyed by process name, gives each process
34	    its own 32-bit positive seed that varies across master_seeds (ensemble
35	    diversity) but is stable per (master_seed, process_name) pair (reproducibility).
36	    Mirrors the pattern already used in v2ecoli/steps/division.py.
37	    """
38	    return binascii.crc32(process_name.encode("utf-8"), master_seed) & 0x7FFFFFFF

## [2026-06-09T22:46:00.564Z] assistant

Task #3f done — **Task #3 complete in its entirety**. Two commits:
- `bbafc02` — composite arch + 9 tests
- `9e79580` — HANDOFF pointer bump

## What landed

**`v2ecoli/composites/kinetic_charging_baseline.py`** (~115 LOC) — registers `kinetic_charging_baseline` via `@composite_generator`. Sibling of `baseline.py` that swaps `SteadyStatePolypeptideElongation` → `KineticTrnaChargingPolypeptideElongation`; everything else matches `baseline` tick-for-tick.

**Pragmatic delegation:** rather than copy 600+ LOC of orchestration (like `millard_pdmp_baseline.py`'s 832 LOC), this delegates to `baseline()` via a scoped `PARTITIONED_PROCESSES` swap through a `try/finally` context manager. Original mapping is restored even on exception, blocking dirty-mutation leak. Documented as future-work-able for a proper refactor.

**Registration:** appended to `composites/__init__.py` (imports + `__all__`) and to `cache_version.INPUT_FILES`.

## Tests

`tests/test_behavior_kinetic_charging.py` — 9 tests:
- **7 smoke** (all pass): module imports, function signature, registry entry by name, swap helper preserves and restores, exception-safety, `INPUT_FILES` membership, `__all__` export.
- **2 cache-gated**: stale-cache-build succeeds (passes — exercises the Task #5 soft-fail path); one-tick-run gated on Task #8 (skipped).

**Results:** `pytest tests/test_behavior_kinetic_charging.py` → 8 passed, 1 skipped in 5 s. All kinetic-charging fast tests: 68 passed in 3.16 s.

## Task #3 grand summary

| File | LOC | Sub-tasks |
|---|---:|---|
| `v2ecoli/processes/polypeptide/kinetic_charging.py` | ~975 | 3a–3d |
| `v2ecoli/composites/kinetic_charging_baseline.py` | ~115 | 3f |
| `v2ecoli/library/sim_data.py` extension | ~95 | Task #5 |
| Tests | ~1200 | All sub-tasks |

## Remaining plan

| Task | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| #4 — other process deltas | pending | independent, lower-priority for shipping kinetic |
| #8 — full ParCa rerun | pending | unblocks behavior-tick test + #9–#13 |
| #9 — cache rebuild | pending | auto via `scripts/build_cache.py` |
| #10 — fast tests | pending | already running clean (68 passing) |
| #11 — behavior tests | pending | gates on #8 |
| #12 — parity gate | pending | expected to *not* be bit-identical for the new arch |
| #13 — reports | pending | hours of compute |

**Next session:** Task #8 (full ParCa rerun) — purely compute, can run in the background. After it completes, the one-tick behavior test activates automatically and #9–#13 cascade.

## [2026-06-09T22:51:59.815Z] user


<details><summary>tool result</summary>

```
1	# Generate full-mode ParCa fixture
2	
3	Instructions for a Claude session to produce a production-quality
4	`parca_state.pkl.gz` that supports full simulation (not just comparison
5	testing).
6	
7	## Context
8	
9	The shipped `models/parca/parca_state.pkl.gz` was built with `--mode fast`
10	(debug=True, reduced TF condition set).  This is sufficient for comparison
11	testing and the ParCa comparison report, but the online simulation's
12	equilibrium solver crashes on it because some metabolite concentrations are
13	left unpopulated when only a subset of TF conditions are fitted.
14	
15	A `--mode full` run produces a complete sim_data with all ~300 TF conditions
16	fitted.  This takes several hours but the result supports end-to-end
17	simulation (single cell → division → daughters).
18	
19	## Steps
20	
21	### 1. Build Cython extensions
22	
23	```bash
24	cd /Users/eranagmon/code/v2ecoli   # or wherever the repo lives
25	bash scripts/parca_cython_build.sh
26	```
27	
28	This compiles `_build_sequences`, `_fastsums`, and `mc_complexation` for
29	the current Python.  Only needed once per Python version.
30	
31	### 2. Run the full ParCa pipeline
32	
33	```bash
34	python scripts/parca_run.py --mode full --cpus 8 -o out/sim_data
35	```
36	
37	**Expected runtime:** 4–8 hours depending on CPU count.  Step 5
38	(fit_condition) dominates — it solves a fixed-point iteration for each of
39	~300 conditions.  Steps 1–4 take ~10 min; steps 6–9 take ~2 min.
40	
41	The script emits per-step checkpoints at `out/sim_data/checkpoint_step_N.pkl`
42	and a final `out/sim_data/parca_state.pkl`.  If step 5 succeeds but a later
43	step crashes, you can resume:
44	
45	```bash
46	python scripts/parca_run.py --mode full --cpus 8 \
47	  --resume-from-step 6 --resume-pickle out/sim_data/checkpoint_step_5.pkl
48	```
49	
50	### 3. Package the fixture
51	
52	```bash
53	gzip -c out/sim_data/parca_state.pkl > models/parca/parca_state.pkl.gz
54	cp out/sim_data/runtimes.json models/parca/runtimes.json
55
…[truncated 2816 chars]
```
</details>

## [2026-06-09T22:52:16.766Z] user


<details><summary>tool result</summary>

```
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Build the 3 Cython extensions vendored under
# v2ecoli/processes/parca/wholecell/utils/ for the current Python.
#
# Run once after a fresh clone or after Python/numpy upgrade.  Output is
# three .so files alongside the .pyx sources (gitignored).
set -euo pipefail
REPO_ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
PY="${PYTHON:-${REPO_ROOT}/.venv/bin/python}"
cd "$REPO_ROOT"
"$PY" -c "import Cython" 2>/dev/null || "$PY" -m pip install cython
"$PY" - <<'PY'
import numpy as np, sys
from Cython.Build import cythonize
from setuptools import Extension
from distutils.core import setup as dsetup
base = 'v2ecoli/processes/parca/wholecell/utils'
mods = ('_build_sequences', 'mc_complexation', '_fastsums')
exts = [Extension(f'v2ecoli.processes.parca.wholecell.utils.{m}',
                  [f'{base}/{m}.pyx'],
                  include_dirs=[np.get_include()],
                  define_macros=[('NPY_NO_DEPRECATED_API','NPY_1_7_API_VERSION')])
        for m in mods]
sys.argv = ['build', 'build_ext', '--inplace']
dsetup(name='v2ecoli-parca-cython', ext_modules=cythonize(exts, language_level=3))
PY
echo "done: $(ls $REPO_ROOT/v2ecoli/processes/parca/wholecell/utils/*.so | wc -l) .so files built"
```
</details>

## [2026-06-09T23:48:47.776Z] assistant

ParCa is making progress — Steps 1–3 completed in 18 s combined; "Starting 10 Parca processes" means Step 4 (fit_condition) is underway. Per the docs that step dominates total runtime (~4–8 h). 

My monitor will wake me when either the process exits or ≥5 step events accumulate. Reporting status:

## Status

**Three pre-flight bugs caught and fixed before the run took off:**

1. **`MoleculeIds.start_codon` missing** — needed for Relation's codon methods. Added `"start_codon": "start"` to `molecule_ids.py` dict (1 line).
2. **3 proteins skipped in `_codon_sequences`** — v2ecoli's KB has 3 proteins (`EG11357-MONOMER`, `EG11413-MONOMER`, `MONOMER0-4391`) whose translated mRNA doesn't match their protein sequence; the upstream `_build_codon_based_translation` indexes the dict unconditionally → KeyError. Fixed with `.get(mid, [start_codon])` sentinel so the 3 proteins occupy a 1-codon row instead of crashing. They're not biologically translatable but ParCa fitting proceeds.
3. **tRNA ID convention mismatch in `setInitialRnaExpression`** — v2ecoli's `ids_tRNA_cistrons` uses cistron form (`alaT-tRNA`), but my Relation port produces `[c]`-suffixed tRNA IDs (`alaT-tRNA[c]`). Stripped the suffix when building the lookup dict.

## Run in progress

- PID `46992`, started 19:47:29.
- Step 1 (5.1 s), Step 2 (0.1 s), Step 3 (12.5 s) all done.
- Step 4 underway — 10 parallel ParCa processes spawned. Per docs, ~30 min to complete.
- Step 5 (fit_condition) is the long one — ~9 conditions × ~6 min each, mostly parallel → ~1–2 h total.
- Steps 6–9 fast (~2 min combined).

When the monitor notifies me of completion (success or failure), I'll resume. Estimated total ~2–4 h.

## [2026-06-10T00:39:46.607Z] assistant

Task #8 + #9 complete. Two commits:
- `c6500f5` — full ParCa fixture + cache rebuild + 7 runtime-bug fixes; end-to-end one-tick test passes
- `a2b00e0` — HANDOFF pointer bump

## Headline

**The full kinetic-charging port is now end-to-end live.** The behavior test that was gated on Task #8 (`test_composite_runs_one_tick_with_kinetic_elongation`) now **passes** — the kinetic composite builds against the fresh cache, runs one tick without crashing, ribosome counts stay positive.

## What ran

| Phase | Time |
|---|---|
| Full ParCa (10 CPUs) | 194.3 s (docs estimated 4–8 h — written for a slower box) |
| Fixture packaging | ~10 s |
| Cache rebuild | ~15 s |

## Bugs caught + fixed mid-flight (7 total)

**3 in the ParCa pipeline** — gaps left by Task #6's Relation port not fully wiring through v2ecoli's KB:
- `MoleculeIds.start_codon` missing (1-line add).
- 3 proteins (`EG11357-MONOMER`, `EG11413-MONOMER`, `MONOMER0-4391`) with mRNA-vs-protein mismatches → defensive `.get(mid, [start_codon])` sentinel.
- tRNA ID convention mismatch in `setInitialRnaExpression` (cistron form vs `[c]`-suffixed) → strip the suffix when building the lookup dict.

**4 in the kinetic Process port** — only surfaced once the one-tick test could actually run:
- `get_kinetic_constants` regressed on pint Quantities (v2ecoli passes Quantity, upstream assumes plain float).
- `max_charging_rate` shape mismatch — `k_cat__per_s` is per-AA (21), `synthetase_idx` covers 22 enzymes. Projected via `aa_from_synthetase @ counts`.
- `request` returned per-tRNA (86) `fraction_charged` but v2ecoli base listener expects per-AA (21). Aggregated via `aa_from_trna @ charged` over total.
- `protein_maturation`'s `float()` choked on shape-(1,) ndarrays from `counts(...)`. Coerced via `float(np.asarray(...).sum())`.

## End-to-end verification

`load_cache_bundle('out/cache')["configs"]["ecoli-polypeptide-elongation"]` now contains all 12 kinetic config keys with realistic shapes:

| Key | Shape |
|---|---|
| `codon_sequences` | (4309, 2409) int8 |
| `k_cat__per_s` | (21,) float64 |
| `K_M_trna__per_L` | (86,) float64 |
| `trnas_to_codons` | (63, 86) int8 |
| ... | (etc.) |

**Results:** `pytest tests/test_kinetic_charging_*.py tests/test_behavior_kinetic_charging.py` → **72 passed in 10.94 s**.

## Remaining plan

| Task | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| #4 — other process deltas | pending | independent, not needed for kinetic-charging end-to-end |
| #10 — fast tests | pending | check broader `pytest -m 'not sim'` against the new cache |
| #11 — behavior tests | pending | gating against the new cache; expect some drift in steady-state baseline since the fixture changed |
| #12 — parity gate | pending | expected to *not* be bit-identical (we shipped a new ParCa fixture) |
| #13 — reports | pending | hours of compute |

**Next session:** Task #10 (run `pytest -m 'not sim'` to see what else moves with the new cache).

- `4f910fa` — audit + HANDOFF docs

## Result

`pytest -m 'not sim and not slow'` → **338 passed, 2 failed, 20 skipped** in 33.9 s.

**Both failures are pre-existing on `main`** (verified by checkout) — environmental issue, `ImportError: ParquetEmitter override set but [parquet] extra not installed`. The branch didn't touch either test file. Fix per the message: `pip install 'v2ecoli[parquet]'`.

Affected tests:
- `test_analysis_runner.py::test_run_workflow_runs_analyses_end_to_end`
- `test_workflow_smoke.py::test_tiny_sweep_runs_to_completion`

## What this means

The new ParCa fixture + cache + kinetic-charging port **don't regress any of the 338 unit/structural tests**. Tests that build composites against the cache — `test_baseline_overrides`, `test_visualizations_multigeneration`, `test_polypeptide_elongation_parity`, etc. — all pass.

| Task | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|

| #12 — parity gate | pending | expected to *not* be bit-identical (we shipped a new ParCa fixture) |
| #13 — reports | pending | hours of compute |

**Next session:** Task #11 — behavior tests against the new cache. The 7 PR-gating behavior tests in `tests/test_model_behavior.py` will run baseline + check growth-rate / ribosome / metabolism behavior. Expect some thresholds to need adjustment because the fixture changed.

---tests 1-4---

def test_baseline_dry_mass_roughly_doubles(single_cell_trajectory):
    """Over a complete cycle the cell accumulates ~1.5-2.2x initial dry mass.

    Tight bound: rejects both stalls (ratio < 1.5) and runaway growth
    (> 2.5). Loose enough to survive normal numerical drift."""
    meta = single_cell_trajectory['meta']
    m0 = float(meta['initial_dry_mass'])
    m1 = float(meta['final_dry_mass'])
    ratio = m1 / m0
    assert 1.5 <= ratio <= 2.5, (
        f'Dry-mass ratio {ratio:.2f} outside [1.5, 2.5]. '
        f'initial={m0:.1f}fg final={m1:.1f}fg — cell either stalled or '
        f'grew runaway.')


def test_baseline_monotone_growth(single_cell_trajectory):
    """Growth trajectory is roughly monotone — no long flat spans.

    Allow small downward blips (bulk counter noise, rounding) but no
    >60 s interval where dry_mass is strictly non-increasing."""
    snaps = single_cell_trajectory['snapshots']
    masses = [(s['time'], s['dry_mass']) for s in snaps if 'dry_mass' in s]
    if len(masses) < 4:
        pytest.skip('need at least 4 mass snapshots')

    # Sliding window: over any 4 consecutive snaps, last > first.
    for i in range(len(masses) - 3):
        t0, m0 = masses[i]
        t3, m3 = masses[i + 3]
        assert m3 > m0, (
            f'Dry mass did not grow over window [{t0:.0f}s..{t3:.0f}s]: '
            f'{m0:.1f} -> {m3:.1f} fg. A stall of this length means a '
            f'listener crashed or metabolism shut off.')

"""Definitive behavioral tests for the baseline whole-cell model.

These tests pin the four properties that define a working simulation:

  1. Baseline cell grows. Dry mass roughly doubles over a cell cycle.
  2. Chromosome replication initiates at the expected time (~22 min after
     start, yielding 2 chromosomes before division).
  3. Division fires: the pre-division cell splits into two daughters with
     bulk conservation.
  4. Daughters grow: after division, both daughters are viable and their
     dry mass increases over a short follow-on run.

Tests resume from the pre-division checkpoint produced by `reports/workflow_report.py`
(see tests/conftest.py). This avoids the ~4 min warmup and lets the tests
run in seconds — suitable to block PR merges, not just nightly runs.

If the checkpoint is absent, tests skip. CI should either:
  - produce the checkpoint in a setup job and upload as an artifact, or
  - pull a blessed checkpoint from release assets / LFS.
"""
import pytest
from v2ecoli.library.quantity_helpers import fg_magnitude


pytestmark = pytest.mark.behavior


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

"""
v2ecoli Workflow Testing Framework

Step-based pipeline that caches intermediate states, replacing benchmark_report.py.
Each step checks for cached metadata before executing, enabling incremental
development and fast re-runs.

Pipeline Steps:
0. biocyc — Fetch raw data files from the EcoCyc API
1. raw_data — Catalog raw TSV files and knowledge base stats
2. parca — Run parameter calculator (ParCa) or load cached simData
3. load_model — Build composite from cache
4. single_cell — Run single cell to division
5. division — Cell division, conservation, daughter viability
6. daughters — Divide and run both daughters

Usage:
    python reports/workflow_report.py              # run full pipeline
    python reports/workflow_report.py --clean      # clear cache and re-run

Rendering has been migrated to WorkflowVisualization (v2ecoli/visualizations/workflow.py).
This wrapper keeps all pipeline orchestration and dispatches to the Step for HTML.
"""

        print(f" ({build_time:.2f}s)")

## What's happening

`reports/workflow_report.py` is running a full single-cell lifecycle to division (~42 min wall clock). When it completes it'll emit `single_cell_meta.json` with `chromosome_snapshots`, unlocking the 4 currently-skipped behavior tests:
- `test_baseline_dry_mass_roughly_doubles`
- `test_baseline_monotone_growth`
- `test_replication_completes_in_expected_window`
- `test_replication_forks_present_mid_cycle`

## Patch made

`reports/workflow_report.py` had **27 `float(mass.get('X', ...))`** call sites that crashed against the new cache (post-Task-#8 listeners emit pint Quantities, not bare floats). Added a `_fg(value)` helper at the top that:
- detects `hasattr(value, 'to')` → converts via `.to(units.fg).magnitude`
- bare numbers pass through

Then bulk-replaced `float(mass.get(` → `_fg(mass.get(` and `float(cell.get(` → `_fg(cell.get(` (27 occurrences total). Same pattern as the Task #8 fix #4 in `kinetic_charging.py`.

Will resume when the workflow_report completes.

## [2026-06-10T01:03:15.799Z] assistant

Task #11 done. Two commits:
- `e8c5224` — workflow_report patches + audit/HANDOFF
- `0995f91` — HANDOFF pointer bump

## Headline result

**All 7 PR-gating behavior tests PASS against the new cache** — no threshold adjustments needed.

| Test | Status |
|---|:-:|
| `test_baseline_dry_mass_roughly_doubles` | ✅ ratio ∈ [1.5, 2.5] |
| `test_baseline_monotone_growth` | ✅ no flat 60s+ windows |
| `test_replication_completes_in_expected_window` | ✅ 2nd chrom within [900, 1800] s |
| `test_replication_forks_present_mid_cycle` | ✅ |
| `test_division_splits_cell_into_two_daughters` | ✅ |
| `test_division_conserves_unique_molecules` | ✅ |
| `test_daughters_build_and_grow` | ✅ |

The trajectory came out clean: **division at t=2491 s, dry_mass=702.8 fg, 2 chromosomes, 11.9× realtime**. The new fixture is biologically valid.

## What had to be patched first

The 4 trajectory-dependent tests need `out/workflow/single_cell_meta.json`, produced by `python reports/workflow_report.py`. The new Task #8 cache emits `listeners.mass.*` as pint Quantities, but the report script had ~27 `float(mass.get(...))` call sites that crashed with `DimensionalityError`.

**Patches:**
- Added `_fg(value)` helper at the top of `reports/workflow_report.py` — same pattern as the Task #8 fix #4 in `kinetic_charging.py`.
- Bulk-replaced `float(mass.get(` → `_fg(mass.get(` (~25 sites) and `float(cell.get(` → `_fg(cell.get(` (~2 sites) via a Python sed pass.
- One residual `float(d_mass.get('dry_mass', 0))` in `_run_daughter` (Step 4) survives; surfaces only in daughter simulations, not in the path needed by the behavior tests. Deferred to Task #13.

## Remaining plan

| Task | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| #4 — other process deltas | pending | independent |
| #12 — parity gate | next | expected to *not* be bit-identical (fixture changed); the test exists to detect *unintentional* drift |
| #13 — reports | pending | finish the `_run_daughter` cleanup + render the trna-specific report |

**Next session:** Task #12 — `scripts/parity_check.py` against the committed `tests/golden/baseline_parity_signature.json`. Because we shipped a new ParCa fixture, the parity result is expected to differ; the test documents intentional drift rather than gating it.

## [2026-06-10T01:06:22.069Z] assistant

Task #12 done. Two commits:
- `34cade6` — audit + HANDOFF
- `21a54d7` — HANDOFF pointer bump

## Result

**Both gates ran:**

| Gate | Outcome |
|---|---|
| Real-emitter `build_composite` | ✅ OK |
| Null-emitter signature vs golden | Structured drift — expected |

The **real-emitter build-check is the important pass** — it catches emitter-schema resolve regressions that the null-emitter signature would silently miss.

## Signature drift (intentional, from Task #8 fixture refresh)

```
bulk_sum         Δ +0.30%
cell_mass        Δ +0.30%  (1294.77 → 1298.68 fg)
dry_mass         Δ +0.31%  (388.63 → 389.82 fg)
listeners_n      Δ +0.28%  (91884 → 92138)
listeners_sum    Δ +10%
active_ribosome  +170      (13542 → 13712)
active_RNAP      +83
RNA              +33
```

All structured, all ~tenths of a percent on the bulk/mass side. Ratios and relationships preserved. No process is going off the rails (no 10× drift on any one count).

## Why not re-capture the golden

Per AGENTS.md:
> Re-capture the golden (`--out`) only from a clean `origin/main` worktree when main's behavior intentionally changes.

The drift is intentional but the golden lives on `main`. Re-capturing on this branch would invite a confused PR review. The PR reviewer will see the audit and re-capture from `main` after merge — standard "fixture refresh → re-capture" cycle.

## Remaining plan

| Task | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| #4 — other process deltas | pending | independent |
| #13 — reports | next | finish `_run_daughter` cleanup + render the tRNA-specific report |

**Next session:** Task #13 — generate `workflow_report.html`, `multigeneration_report.html`, and the dedicated tRNA-charging HTML with provenance banner. First step: fix the residual `_run_daughter` `float(...)` bug that was deferred from Task #11.

scripts/pr_session_report.py
---
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Standard PR session report for v2ecoli.

Generates a self-describing, rich HTML report for a PR / work session:

  * a **provenance banner** (timestamp, git SHA/branch, dirty badge, last
    commit, generator path, host/OS/Python);
  * **metric cards** (parity verdict, commits, files changed);
  * **before/after parity plots** — the same short baseline simulation run on
    two git refs (e.g. ``main`` vs the feature branch), overlaid, so a reviewer
    can see at a glance whether a refactor preserved behavior;
  * an optional **mass-conservation panel** (residual + exchange trajectories,
    when the capture enabled the ``mass_conservation`` feature);
  * the PR's **commit list** and a free-text **summary**.

Standard way to attach evidence to a substantial PR (see AGENTS.md "HTML
reports with provenance banners").

Usage
-----
    python scripts/pr_session_report.py capture --out /tmp/after.json --steps 60
    cp scripts/pr_session_report.py /tmp/prr.py            # so it exists on the base ref
    git checkout main && python /tmp/prr.py capture --out /tmp/before.json --steps 60
    git checkout -
    python scripts/pr_session_report.py render --before /tmp/before.json \
```
</details>

v2ecoli multigeneration report. Runs a single cell to division, keeps exactly
one daughter, runs that to division, keeps one of its daughters, etc. — for a
configurable number of generations. Dispatches the concatenated trajectory
(rows tagged by ``generation``) to
v2ecoli.visualizations.multigeneration.MultigenerationVisualization. python

  -h, --help            show this help message and exit
  --study STUDY         Path to a v3-shape study.yaml driving the lineage.
                        When set, the baseline entry's params and the lineage
                        block (generations, max_duration) become defaults; CLI
                        flags below still override.
  --generations GENERATIONS
                        Number of generations to run (default: 3, or
                        study.lineage.generations).
  --max-duration MAX_DURATION
                        Safety cap (seconds) per generation (default: 3600, or
                        study.lineage.max_duration).
  --out OUT             Output HTML path.
  --seed SEED
  --cache-dir CACHE_DIR
                        Directory holding ParCa cache (default: out/cache or
                        out/workflow/cache, or study param).
```
